# QUESTIONS from flows-audit
This file holds every question the audit asked the lead, with the lead's answer. It was restructured on 2026-11-16 after the style read (reviews/style-questions-r1.md). Nothing was deleted: text that no longer applies sits under the last heading, "History (superseded, kept)". The current rule for each question stays where the question is. Times: Z = UTC; IST = UTC + 5 h 30 min.

## STATUS INDEX
Every question has exactly one status. OPEN = no answer from the lead in ORDERS.md yet. ANSWERED = the lead answered and nothing is waiting on the lead. ANSWERED, lead action pending = the lead answered, and the lead (or the owner, through the lead) still has a step to do; each step is one row of the LEAD ACTIONS table below. A question with several items gets the status of the whole question; each item carries its own label inside, from this list only: [ANSWERED], [ANSWERED, lead action pending], [SUPERSEDED] (the item no longer applies; the replacement is named), [PARTLY SUPERSEDED] (one part no longer applies; the part that stands is written out), [OPEN] (no answer from the lead yet; used on every open item of Q31), [NOTE] (an index or a comment that asks nothing and has no answer; counted apart from [OPEN]; Q31 item 66 and items 41, 42, 44, 45 and 46 of the second list carry it), and UNVERIFIED (stated from a file or from the lead's words, not run or seen by the audit). A label may be followed by a bracketed explanation in round brackets; a label is never joined to another label with a semicolon or a comma other than the one inside "ANSWERED, lead action pending". "The style read" = the earlier plain-wording review of this file. "The owner sheet" = the owner's step sheet, the file OWNER-STEPS-q16-rows.md; its numbered "items" and table rows are meant whenever this file says "owner sheet item N". "Ownersteps7" and "ownersteps8" = the logs fix-ownersteps7.md and fix-ownersteps8.md, where two earlier fixers first wrote the Q31 questions. "ORDERS.md line N" means the line of the lead's answer text; the heading of that block is the line before. ORDERS.md lines 109-171 hold the answers to Q13 and later; older answers sit in the ORDER blocks named in the table. Line numbers checked against ORDERS.md on 2026-11-16 (answers start at line 109; 173 holds ORDER 035; 176 holds ORDER 037, whose item 3 is on line 177; the Q30 block is lines 169-171: heading on 169, text on 170-171, its items 1 to 3 on line 171; "ORDERS.md lines 170-171" names the whole Q30 answer, "line 171" only its items). ORDER 037 item 3 is an answer from the lead, not a question: it has its own section below, "ORDER 037 ITEM 3". Every default in this file that is not in the lead's own words stays "planned by the audit, lead to confirm"; the audit invents no answer of the lead. Several words used early in this file ("take path", "send", "paid call", "take prep", "take-prep message", "ceiling announcement", "delete message 1", "delete message 2", "re-pin", "practice run", "rehearsal") are explained in the GLOSSARY section below; look there first if a word is unclear.

## GATE F-011 AND F-041 (one statement; this replaces every other wording in this file)
What the lead said, in order of the lines of ORDERS.md (a later line is newer; the time stamps on those blocks are wrong, see Q30): line 132, item 3 (Q21): F-011 and F-041 "stay Major until a fix and a test exist (never downgrade by counting)". Line 156, item 9 (Q27): F-011 and F-041 "are take path: they must be fixed or downgraded with evidence before Tue 14:00Z, otherwise automatic NO-GO".
The two lines differ: line 132 allows only a fix with a test; line 156 also allows a downgrade with evidence. The rule here is line 156, because it is the newer line and the lead wrote it as the answer to the question "are they part of the take path". Counting never downgrades them (line 132, still stands).
THE GATE ("take path" here = everything the take depends on: the take scripts and their tests and the flow-file and config ledger rows flagged TAKE-PATH: yes and counted by GO row 6, plus F-011 and F-041 by name; full entry in the GLOSSARY): before Tue 17 Nov 14:00Z (19:30 IST), each of F-011 and F-041 is either (a) fixed, with a test that fails on the old text or code and passes on the new, or (b) downgraded with written evidence agreed by a second person who is a human (Q31 item 10). If neither is done for one of them, the GO/NO-GO of that time is NO-GO.
Two facts the lead has not settled, so they are not invented here: (1) WHO must act is NOT NAMED anywhere in ORDERS.md lines 109-171 (UNVERIFIED that anyone has been given the job). (2) What counts as "evidence" for a downgrade is not written. The lead must supply both in Q31 item 10 (and action L24) by Tue 17 Nov 06:15Z (11:45 IST), about 1 h 45 min before the planned end of the re-pin event (08:00Z; RUNBOOK step 13 starts 07:14Z on RUNBOOK's 04:00Z clock) and about 7 h 45 min before the deadline of Tue 17 Nov 14:00Z (19:30 IST) (planned by the audit, lead to confirm); without that answer nobody can clear the two findings in time and the GO/NO-GO is NO-GO. THE PASS CRITERION is a question for the lead, not a decision of the audit. The audit's recommended wording: "A downgrade of F-011 or F-041 counts as evidence only if it (1) names the file and line that show the take path does not meet the problem, (2) is backed by a command output or a file the reader can open, and (3) is read and agreed by a second person (who that is: Q31 item 10 only; this GATE does not repeat that wording). A fix counts only with a test that fails on the old text or code and passes on the new." Is that the standard? The real fix for F-011 (FIX-PLAN item 8, moving n8n's own records) waits until after the video (Q4), so before Tue 14:00Z only (b), or a fix of the wording for F-041, can clear them. F-041 is wording ("one database" said in 15 ways), so a text fix with a test is possible; F-011 is a design fact and is likely to need (b). F-011 ROUTE POINT (round 12; planned by the audit, lead to confirm): TAKE-CHECKLIST.md Inputs item 22 needs the lead's choice of fix (A) or downgrade (B) by Tue 17 Nov 03:00Z (08:30 IST) (Q31 item 10 (a)), because fix A needs n8n's database settings at the FIRST n8n start (RUNBOOK step 7, 05:39Z on the 04:00Z clock, 05:10Z on the 03:31Z clock) and a later A means restarting n8n and repeating RUNBOOK steps 11 to 13. The audit sets the answer-by at Tue 17 Nov 03:00Z = 08:30 IST (Q31 item 10 part (a)); if it is late or missing, fix A is dropped and F-011 goes the downgrade route (B). The 14:00Z in the GATE is the deadline for the result, not for this choice.

## GLOSSARY (plain words; every code and word the style read named)
| Word or code | Plain meaning |
|---|---|
| the lead | the Claude session on the owner's laptop that prepares and runs the demo and writes ORDERS.md. Never called "coordinator" (Q25 item 9). Not to be confused with "a lead" below. |
| a lead (a record) | one made-up person's form submission that the demo handles. Its identity is the lead key ("lead ID" on screen). One word, two meanings: "the lead" is the session, "a lead" is a record. |
| the owner | the studio owner, the human who decides, types a few things on camera and posts the video. |
| the audit | this cloud session, "flows-audit", which writes this file and reviews the n8n flows. |
| n8n | the ready-made workflow tool whose flows the demo runs. A flow is a chain of steps (nodes). |
| Postgres, mf_leads | Postgres is the database. mf_leads is its table of incoming leads ("the incoming-people table"). |
| HubSpot trial, CRM | the real customer-record service, used through a free trial account with made-up people only. CRM = customer record service. |
| take | the one recorded run of the demo for the video, scenes 1 to 4 of the run sheet. Also "the take folder" = where its scripts live. Whether there is a retake is not decided in ORDERS.md lines 109-171 (UNVERIFIED); OWNER-STEPS-q16-rows.md (the entry 'A retake' and the lines that name the retake) describe a retake as a second recorded run if a check fails. |
| real-v5, film-v5 | two folders of flow files. real-v5 is what the take uses; film-v5 holds stand-in scenes 5-11, which are not in this video (decision 1). |
| scene 4 | the scene where n8n is stopped on purpose and another run takes the lead over. It is filmed only if the rehearsal shows the takeover inside 3 minutes (45 s lease + 30 s check = about 75 s, plus margin) AND only if the lead has supplied the stop script, its trigger and its timing (Q31 item 105) and the sentence of Q31 item 80; without them scene 4 is cut (Q31 items 28, 80 and 105). K1, K2, K3, K3B are the labels of the stop points the test harness uses (CLAIMS-TABLE.md, the glossary entry "K1, K2, K3, K3B"). |
| decision N | item N of inputs/DECISIONS-2026-11-16.md (12 numbered decisions). "Decision 1" = the one video is real-v5 only, scenes 1-4. |
| Blocker, Major, Nit | severity of a finding: Blocker = the take cannot run or would say something false; Major = a real defect the take may hit; Nit = does not matter for the take. |
| F-002 | finding: a lead with a missing key is not rejected and a failed database write answers the sender with a bare error 500 (FINDINGS.md row F-002). Blocker until tests T19 to T21 pass on Wednesday (ORDERS.md line 156 item 9 and line 171 item 1; a failure of T19 or T20 on the patched copy means NO-GO, Q29 item 3). This is the only meaning of "F-002" in this file. |
| F-002b, Claim, alwaysOutputData | F-002b = the fallback patch of F-002 (FINDINGS.md row F-002). Claim = the n8n step named Claim in mf-form.json and mf-hook.json that records that a lead has been taken. alwaysOutputData = an n8n step setting that lets a step pass on an empty result instead of stopping; the patch keeps it on Claim (the audit's reading of FINDINGS.md, UNVERIFIED by the lead). |
| F-018 | finding: runtime-only behaviour is unproven ($env in expressions, HubSpot answer shape, SMTP and stage timeouts, whether DESK_ALERT_TO is set) (FINDINGS.md row F-018). Rated Major since round 16Z; its evidence is that the error branches go to OutStage (the n8n step that calls the stage program) before MailClaim (the step that claims the mail). This is the only meaning of "F-018" here; Q21 item 1 "env access" is one of its parts. |
| HELD, T-ENV | HELD = a run-sheet row not pasted as fact until the lead answers (default safe text used) (Q22b). T-ENV = the one test that reads a $env value inside a node (Q21 item 1). |
| missing: GO or NO-GO, recording-ready message, the chase | "missing: GO or NO-GO" = the owner's reply (written once) at Tue 17 Nov 16:00Z (21:30 IST) if no GO or NO-GO message has come. [PARTLY SUPERSEDED by Q31 item 6 (b) and the Q31 front page (AUDIT PROPOSAL, lead to confirm): the lead's own deadline to send the GO or NO-GO message is Tue 17 Nov 15:00Z (20:30 IST); 16:00Z (21:30 IST) is the owner's cut-off to acknowledge a GO and the time from which the owner writes "missing: GO or NO-GO"; only a one-line reply that says the GO message was read counts as "read"; the words "missing: GO or NO-GO" say that NO message has come, so they count as no reply and never as an acknowledgement; a GO not sent by 15:00Z is NO-GO (a GO that was never sent cannot have been read); a GO with no "read" reply by 16:00Z is treated as NO-GO.] Recording-ready message = the lead's message on the take day that says where the recording is and names the editing tool and method (OWNER-STEPS-q16-rows.md item 5). "The chase" = the owner's reply "missing: recording-ready message", due at or after Wed 13:05Z (18:35 IST) if that message has not come, even if only the retake question has (AUDIT PROPOSAL, Q31 item 24 (f): after the 13:00Z (18:30 IST) post-rule time of item 24 (d) and after the latest reply to a retake question, about 13:02Z (18:32 IST) in Timing case 2, so a chase never comes before the deadline it chases; the owner sheet says 12:30Z, a follow-up for the owner sheet). |
| F-007 | finding: the kill-case names K3 and K3B differ between the page statements (the file n8n-flows/PAGE-STATEMENTS-V4.md, the numbered written claims about what the demo does; "page" in this file always means that file), the run sheet and the test harness (FINDINGS.md row F-007). Blocker; a text fix. |
| F-008 | finding: no error workflow, six recovery steps swallow errors, run saving was set to none (FINDINGS.md row F-008). Its patch turns run saving on. |
| F-011 | finding: n8n keeps its own state (workflows, credentials, run history with lead name and e-mail) in its own file database, not in the take's Postgres (FINDINGS.md row F-011). UNVERIFIED that the file is SQLite: the audit read it from one earlier trial run (CLAIMS-TABLE.md, the line "n8n runs on its own database today (a small SQLite file)"), and the lead records the fact at the re-smoke (L1, Q4). Major; cleared only under the GATE above. |
| F-013 | finding: a stage call that never finishes ends the lead as failed after 3 tries (FINDINGS.md row F-013). Nit; accepted as a trade-off. |
| F-031 | finding: a lead with an invalid e-mail address ends in state review and nothing alerts on it (FINDINGS.md row F-031). Blocker; a text fix (a sentence in the run sheet). |
| F-041 | finding: the audit text says "one database" in 15 different ways (FINDINGS.md row F-041). Major; cleared only under the GATE above. |
| F-044 | finding: the only error-trigger flow is kept but the take has no error workflow (FINDINGS.md row F-044). Two options are written; the lead decides at re-pin. |
| F-005, F-006, F-023, F-012, F-014 ... F-045 | other finding numbers; each is one row of FINDINGS.md (answer by finding number; texts in the FINDINGS.md rows with those numbers). Q9 lists the demoted ones. |
| T19, T20, T21 | three tests of the F-002 patch on the patched real-v5 copy: T19 = the same key twice sends one mail; T20 = an empty or invalid key gives error 422; T21 = the take database unreachable (method: Q31 item 22, ALTER ROLE .. NOLOGIN plus pg_terminate_backend; the earlier "REVOKE CONNECT" method changed nothing, because CONNECT is granted to PUBLIC; stopping the whole Postgres server could also stop n8n's own state if the same server holds it, F-011) gives 503 and a resend works (FINDINGS.md, section 'Verdict per part of the build'; the words "take database unreachable" are a reviewer's, unverified). Run at the end of the practice run, not on Tuesday (Q27 item 9, Q30). |
| S9-2, S9-3 | owner actions the decisions file lists: S9-2 = the owner compares the figures in the take's settings file; S9-3 = the owner revokes the model key at clean-up the same day (Q27 item 10). Where the list "S9" itself lives is not stated in ORDERS.md lines 109-171 (UNVERIFIED). |
| RWQ-1 ... RWQ-9 | questions numbered inside RUNSHEET-WORDING.md. RWQ-1 to RWQ-7 were first called Q25-1 to Q25-7 and were renamed so they do not clash with the lead's Q25 items. RWQ-8 = Q28 item 1 (where the caption says the one message was found). RWQ-9 = Q28 item 2 (counts in frame). |
| A-016f, A-016g, A-016c, A-016e | caption ids in RUNSHEET-WORDING.md. A-016f = the scene 3 caption and the one card line: "In this recording, the same lead ID sent twice gave one e-mail message in the intake mailbox." A-016g = an optional extra spoken line about the limit. A-016c and A-016e = the old broad sentence "processed once", cut. |
| P4a (and P1, P2, P4b, P4c) | five checks on made-up leads. P4a = what the real CRM answers with for a contact's number (an all-digit id or "vid"); P2 = whether n8n saves its runs (CLAIMS-TABLE.md, the glossary entry "P checks"). |
| Q-A, Q-B, Q-H, Q-F | labels of open questions inside other files: Q-A = who stops n8n in scene 4 (the lead, Q22a); Q-B = who starts the stopwatch (the answer "the owner's phone" is SUPERSEDED by Q25 item 12: no phone filming and no on-screen timer; nobody is named to time the 3 minutes, Q31 item 13); Q-H = the re-pin date; Q-F = whether the closing card is used (Q31 item 103). They are explained again in Q22a and Q25 item 4. 'RW Q-A', 'RW Q-D', 'RW Q-G', 'RW Q-I', 'RW Q-J' and 'RWQ-10' are labels of RUNSHEET-WORDING.md (Q31 item 76 (a) to (e); RW Q-A = who types the machine actions of scenes 1 to 3, not the Q-A of Q22a; RW Q-J = the stage-service check, items 73 (c) and 76 (e); RWQ-10 = who speaks the voice rows live, item 100 (a)); this file writes them with 'RW'. |
| Row 4 | one made-up lead sent through the real HubSpot trial before the rehearsal (Q17 item 5, Q26 item 5). Its three parts are "Row 4 part 1, 2 and 3" in OWNER-STEPS-q16-rows.md. Not a table row. |
| Step A | REMOVED. It was an owner step that asked for a new yes with a US$ amount; the owner's standing yes already exists (Q25 item 1, Q28 item 3). Ignore any "Step A (if kept)" text. |
| Step D | the owner's step: delete one made-up contact by hand in HubSpot. One Step D message per leftover contact (OWNER-STEPS-q16-rows.md, the entry 'Step D'). |
| re-pin | one EVENT of 269 minutes (RUNBOOK steps 1 to 15), not a point in time. The lead's words 'about 08:00Z' (13:30 IST) are read as the rough END of the event (Q31 item 107, AUDIT PROPOSAL, lead to confirm); RUNBOOK's own clock starts step 1 at 04:00Z (09:30 IST) and ends 08:29Z (13:59 IST), and this is the default start (Q31 item 32); only a 03:31Z (09:01 IST) start ends the event at 08:00Z (RUNBOOK section 6; TAKE-CHECKLIST Clock; RUNBOOK wins for minutes). "Before the re-pin" in this file means before step 1; "at the re-pin" means by the end of the event; the step clock is in the section RE-PIN AND WEDNESDAY CLOCKS below. Inside the event the lead moves the take to the new core commit (a pin = a fixed commit id), copies the take scripts into the take folder, writes the shas, and checks the take files. The check is inside this event; there is no separate "re-check" (Q25 item 6); the owner-sheet message "re-check done" is sent when the short re-smoke is also finished (planned 09:43Z (15:13 IST); 09:30Z (15:00 IST) only if the Tuesday positive control of RUNBOOK step 16 is dropped [PARTLY SUPERSEDED by Q31 item 6 (a)]; glossary row "re-check done"). |
| re-smoke | a short test on the lead's laptop right after the re-pin: n8n started through the lock guard, flows imported and filled [PARTLY SUPERSEDED (ORDER 037 item 3, ORDERS.md line 177: one filled flow set with the real trial credential in every phase; the audit's reading, lead to confirm: the Tuesday fill is the same real fill, not a separate test fill; when the fill happens is Q31 item 8)], one made-up lead sent twice, counts proof. About 1 hour (ORDERS.md line 160); the earlier 3.5-4.5 hours (ORDERS.md line 147, quoted in History below) is superseded. |
| practice run | Wed 18 Nov from about 06:00Z (11:30 IST): the lead runs the whole take without recording; tests T19-T21 at its end, about 07:00Z (12:30 IST) (Q30 item 1). Order at its end (planned by the audit, lead to confirm): the "safety test" the older text of this file named is exactly tests T19, T20 and T21 (nothing else); they end about 07:00Z (12:30 IST); the take-prep message (L12, L22 [PARTLY SUPERSEDED by Q31 items 27 and 30: under the default the HubSpot token-scopes ask (L22) goes to the owner on Mon 16 Nov night, so the take-prep message carries the ceiling (L12) only]) is sent at the start of Row 4 (07:00Z) and, planned by the audit, lead to confirm, only if T19, T20 and T21 all passed (the audit's earlier default named T19 and T20 only; a failure of T19 or T20 is NO-GO, Q29 item 3; what a T21 failure does: Q31 item 8 point (e)); Row 4 starts only after the ceiling is in the spend ledger, at 07:00Z on the RUNBOOK clock (the take-prep message goes out at the start of Row 4, RUNBOOK step 19). TIME CHECK (RUNBOOK clock controls; an earlier version of this file started Row 4 at 07:05Z and the rehearsal at 07:35Z): Row 4 takes 30 minutes (Q27 item 6; RUNBOOK.md section 8; Q26 item 5 says only that Row 4 comes first) and ends 07:30Z, which is the lead's "about 07:30Z" for the start of the 2-hour rehearsal block (07:30 to 09:30Z). Inside that block: positive-control mail run 07:30 to 07:40Z (L30; delete message 1 lists the 7 contacts that exist after the two control mails are posted and is sent between 07:30Z and 07:40Z, during the mailbox wait of that run, never while Row 4 still runs), rehearsal 1 07:40 to 08:25Z, numbers and recording test 08:25 to 08:40Z, rehearsal 2 08:40 to 09:25Z; then gate and sign-off 09:25 to 09:50Z, pre-take check 09:50 to 09:55Z and the take at 10:00Z at best (10:07Z with the start-state gates, Timing case 1; 11:02Z if RUNBOOK step 24 (f) applies, Timing case 2) (RUNBOOK section 6, TAKE-CHECKLIST Clock). So "07:00Z" for the message means "as soon as the tests end", not a fixed clock time; if the tests end late, the message, Row 4 and everything after it start later by the same time. It needs filled flows (L4) and DESK_ALERT_TO (L7) when it starts, so the fill part of take prep must come before 06:00Z [PARTLY SUPERSEDED (ORDER 037 item 3, ORDERS.md line 177: one filled flow set with the real trial credential in every phase; the alternative "or the practice run uses a test fill" no longer applies)] (Q31 item 8; glossary "take prep"). |
| rehearsal | Wed 18 Nov, rehearsal 1 starts 07:40Z (13:10 IST) after the practice run, Row 4 and the positive-control mail run (the lead's words: about 07:30Z for the start of the 2-hour rehearsal block, Q30 item 1 (whether 'about 07:30Z' means the block or rehearsal 1 is Q31 item 101, unanswered; the RUNBOOK reading is used); RUNBOOK clock, see the section RE-PIN AND WEDNESDAY CLOCKS): a full run-through that measures the waits and the scene 4 takeover time; the lead sets the numbers from it. There are TWO rehearsals (RUNBOOK steps 21 to 23): rehearsal 1 (07:40 to 08:25Z) measures, the lead sets the numbers (08:25 to 08:40Z), rehearsal 2 (08:40 to 09:25Z) runs the exact choices; the pre-take check reads rehearsal 2. Each rehearsal leaves one made-up contact in the real HubSpot trial. | round | two meanings. (1) a review round = one pass of reviewers over a document ("round 2", "r2" in log names). (2) the build rounds r20 to r24 of the take scripts: r20 = test-only, stops load from making the take program refuse; r21 = the take program run_real_check_n8n.py (never writes or deletes in HubSpot, hides secrets, finishes cleanly on signals); r22 = the n8n start and kill scripts (refuse a second n8n for the same take); r23 = a lock-file guard (flock) that replaces r22's scanner, owner yes 2026-11-16; r24 = in progress, its content is not described in ORDERS.md lines 109-171 (UNVERIFIED). None touches the flow files (Q26 item 2). |

(1) the blocker ledger: the file egm\BLOCKER-LEDGER.md (handover/ledger.md while the handover exists) with one row per Blocker or Major; the GO/NO-GO test reads it with ledger_check.py (Q27 item 5); LEDGER-ROWS.md is our copy of rows ready to append. (2) the spend ledger: the record of live model calls that sits next to the stage journal (Q3). |

Machine columns = facts a program can read from the flow file. Judgement columns = the auditor's opinion, filled only where a program comparison with the next version shows a change. |
| GO/NO-GO | the lead's decision on whether the take goes ahead. The dated one is Tue 17 Nov 14:00Z (19:30 IST): GO needs merge, review, final evidence, re-pin and the short re-smoke green, and no open Blocker or Major in the take-path code (scripts and tests, and every ledger row flagged TAKE-PATH: yes in LEDGER-ROWS.md that GO row 6 counts, including the flow-file and config rows F-008, F-018, F-029 and F-037; see "take path" below); F-011 and F-041 follow the GATE above. A second check between rehearsal and take is in Q27 item 7. NO-GO = the take is later. Two limits, which differ on purpose: a slipped take must START by Mon 23 Nov 12:00Z (17:30 IST); this is the limit that governs the slip (L32, L33), because the take itself, the clean-up and a margin must be done before the credits stop. 18:29Z is not a second deadline for the take: it is the moment the owner's prepaid model credits stop (23:59 IST, see "credits stop" below). Nothing paid can run after it. The 12:00Z start limit is the lead's (ORDERS.md line 156, item 7); the margin between 12:00Z and 18:29Z (6 h 29 min) is the lead's choice and is not explained beyond that. Which cut-off decides NO-GO on Tue 17 Nov: see Q31 item 83. TWO CUT-OFFS (planned by the audit, lead to confirm, Q31 item 6): GO on Tue 17 Nov needs the lead's own 14:00Z check (cut-off 1) AND the owner's acknowledgement of the GO message by 16:00Z (21:30 IST) (cut-off 2); silence at 16:00Z turns a GO into NO-GO. The slip rule of L32 and L33 works day by day at the same clock times, until the take starts by Mon 23 Nov 12:00Z (17:30 IST); what a NO-GO on Tuesday sets in motion (the new re-pin day) is Q31 item 84, and the arithmetic of the last slip day is Q31 item 85. |

## RE-PIN AND WEDNESDAY CLOCKS (round 12; RUNBOOK.md section 6 and TAKE-CHECKLIST.md Clock win for minutes; every minute is the audit's estimate, nothing measured)
Rule: where this file and RUNBOOK.md or TAKE-CHECKLIST.md give different minutes, RUNBOOK and TAKE-CHECKLIST control (they are the audit's own run plan). Times below are UTC and IST (IST = UTC + 5 h 30 min). Nothing here is a lead answer; the lead confirms.

TUESDAY 17 NOV, the re-pin event (RUNBOOK steps 1 to 15). Two clocks exist: RUNBOOK's own clock starts step 1 at 04:00Z; the lead's plan "re-pin done about 08:00Z" means step 1 at 03:31Z (29 minutes earlier for every step).
| RUNBOOK step | Starts, 04:00Z clock | Starts, 03:31Z clock | Q31 item or action that needs it first |
|---|---|---|---|

GLOSSARY OF LABELS USED IN THIS LIST (added by fix-q31-4; each reading checked against TAKE-CHECKLIST.md and RUNBOOK.md; planned by the audit, lead to confirm): 'GO row N' = row N (1 to 7) of the table 'Tuesday GO test' in TAKE-CHECKLIST.md: 1 merge, 2 review reports, 3 final evidence (tests and the one mutation batch), 4 re-pin and take check, 5 short re-smoke, 6 no open Blocker or Major in the take-path code (the ledger), 7 F-011 and F-041; the GO at Tue 17 Nov 14:00Z needs all seven to pass. 'Block R1' to 'Block R8' = the blocks of commands in TAKE-CHECKLIST.md that the lead runs to read the pass line of one GO row: R1 row 1; R2 row 2 (R2s counts how often a script report names the script sha; R2j runs only after a person has read every hit); R3 row 3 (R3n for a new head, R3c checks that nothing is left over); R4 row 4 (R4r a repeat, R4b-1 the exported copy, R4f the counts of the second run); R5 row 5 (R5e a fallback post-twice, R5f the count proof); R6 row 6 (R6j once per 'lead decides' row); R7 row 7 (R7j once per listed file); R8 writes the decision GO or NO-GO with who and when. 'Answer cells' = the places named in RUNBOOK.md section 4 ('Answer cells') where the lead copies an answer: a REPLACE_ME word in shas.env, or a file $TAKE_FOLDER/inputs/NAME.sh; this file holds no answers. 'L-numbers' (L1 to L33) = the lead actions, the rows of the LEAD ACTIONS table of this file (for example L20 = provide the runbook and place the take scripts at the re-pin, Tue about 08:00Z; L27 = put the post-twice script and run_real_check_n8n.py on a review branch by Tue 09:00Z). 'TAKE-CHECKLIST row 3 (c)' = part (c) of GO row 3, the one mutation batch. 'W-Tue' and 'W-Wed' = the lead's writing time before step 1 and before Wednesday's practice run, as counted in RUNBOOK.md section 6 ('Writing and review time'): W-Tue 170 minutes, W-Wed 180 minutes. 'I1', 'I18', 'I24', 'I39' (and other I-numbers) = rows of the input table of RUNBOOK.md (section 10): I1 the Tuesday set of take scripts, I18 the Wednesday set, I24 whether the four shas are accepted (item 68), I39 the recording commands (item 72). WORDS THAT HAD MORE THAN ONE READING (added by fix-q31-7; each checked against RUNBOOK.md): 'Row 4' = the first send of Wednesday through the real HubSpot trial, one made-up lead whose run makes a paid model call (RUNBOOK step 19, FIX-PLAN step 10b); earlier runs that only make a made-up HubSpot contact (the Tuesday short re-smoke, T19, T21) are 'contact runs' (item 5) and never 'Row 4'; it is not a row of any table (RUNBOOK's own word "Case" below is not used for it). 'GO row N' (above) = a row of the Tuesday GO test, a different thing. 'Timing case 1' and 'Timing case 2' = the two start-time cases of Wednesday in RUNBOOK section 6 (RUNBOOK itself calls them Case A and Case B in steps 25 and 26); they are also the two chains of RUNBOOK section 8 (item 85): its 'Case A' chain of 367 minutes is Timing case 1 and its 'Case B' chain of 422 minutes is Timing case 2 (the 55 minutes between them are 10 to apply and 45 to repeat step 13); 'Retake case A' and 'Retake case B' = the two ceilings of item 3 (planned sends only, or planned sends plus one retake); the owner sheet's own "Case A" and "Case B" mean the retake pair and are quoted as 'the sheet's Case A'. These are two pairs, Timing case 1 and 2 (RUNBOOK's Case A and B) and Retake case A and B (the sheet's Case A and B); a bare "Case A" is never used in this list outside a quotation. 'The GATE' is the section 'GATE F-011 AND F-041' near the top of this file, and it is GO row 7 of the Tuesday GO test (F-011 and F-041); the whole Tuesday check is 'the Tuesday GO test'. 'Gate' = a pass-or-stop check: the Tuesday GO test (7 rows), the start-state gate (five read-only checks, RUNBOOK step 24) or the gate actor of item 10; the text says which. 'Clock' = planned clock times (RUNBOOK section 6, 'the Clock block'); it never means a cut-off (items 6 and 83). 'Step' = a numbered step of RUNBOOK section 5 (steps 1 to 30, with the parts 4a, 4b and 19a); 'step clock' = the planned start time of such a step; 'step 4a' (the lead's text decisions) and 'step 4b' (the named reader of the pin) are the two parts between steps 4 and 5; 'step 19a' is the positive-control mail run between steps 19 and 20; 'RW' = RUNSHEET-WORDING.md, and 'RW step N' or 'RW table row (x)' use that file's own numbering and lettering, never a RUNBOOK step (fix-q31-16). 'Re-pin event' = RUNBOOK steps 1 to 15, 269 minutes, not a point in time; 'the re-pin end' = its last minute. 'Re-pin' alone = the same Tuesday work (RUNBOOK steps 1 to 15), never the file take-warns.pin; 'the pin' = that file and its reviewer line (item 15; RUNBOOK steps 4b and 13); 'the take' = the one filmed run of Wed 18 Nov (RUNBOOK step 26), and names such as 'take folder', 'take check' and 'take prep' keep the word as a name only. 'Send' (a noun) = one run that makes a paid model call (glossary row 'send, paid call'; item 3); a run that makes a made-up HubSpot contact is a 'contact run' (item 5); the 'positive-control mail run' (item 87) posts two made-up mails, and the audit reads it as no paid call (UNVERIFIED, item 3 point (6)); a note from the lead to the owner is a 'message'. 'The post-twice script' has three meanings: (a) the reviewed file on the review branch (items 58, 70, 75); (b) the Tuesday re-smoke loop that sends one lead twice with the same lead ID (items 25, 53, 86); (c) the positive-control mail run of two different lead IDs (item 87). 'ONE MESSAGE' in an item = the lead answers those items in one reply, in the order written. 'WAIT' HAS TWO MEANINGS (fix-q31-14): 'the mailbox wait' = a number of SECONDS between a send and the reading of the mailbox (WAIT_S; items 25, 45, 50); 'a step wait' = the minutes a step waits for a missing answer (up to 30 minutes, cut by the Tuesday cap of 24 or the Wednesday cap of 28 minutes, item 51); where the text says only 'the wait value' it is the mailbox wait, and where it says 'waits up to 30 minutes' it is a step wait.

WORDS FIXED BY fix-q31-10 (each reading is the audit's, lead to confirm; it answers reviews/q31-reader-r9.md M2, M5, M10): (1) PEOPLE: 'the lead' = the studio's lead Claude session (a program, not a person; where FINDINGS.md says 'the studio lead' it means this same session, never the owner, and it is written 'the lead' here); 'the owner' = the human owner of the studio, who types y, deletes contacts, revokes the key, edits and posts the video (ORDERS.md line 156 item 10; Q27 item 3: 'editing and posting: the owner'); 'the studio owner' is the same person and is written 'the owner' here; 'the take owner login' (item 88) is a DATABASE login, never a person; 'the operator' (the word RUNBOOK.md uses) = whoever runs the RUNBOOK steps on the day, which is the lead, and this list writes 'the lead' (fix-q31-21). (2) 'clock' alone = the planned clock times (see 'Clock' above); a time read from log time stamps is 'the logged time' (item 13; a Claude session holds no stopwatch). (3) TWO CHECKS HAD THE SAME NAME 'pre-take check': 'the Tuesday GO test' = the lead's check of the seven GO rows on Tue 17 Nov 14:00Z (19:30 IST), RUNBOOK step 17; 'the pre-take check' = the lead's five-minute check on Wed 18 Nov between the end of rehearsal 2 and the take, RUNBOOK step 25, rule Q27-7, lead action L32 (09:50 to 09:55Z on the dated schedule, 10:02 to 10:07Z in Timing case 1, 10:57 to 11:02Z in Timing case 2). Older texts that say 'pre-take check' for the second one are renamed in this file only. (4) 'SEND': as a NOUN a send = one run that makes a paid model call; 'to send a message' (verb) = a note from the lead to the owner; 'to post a lead' = to put a made-up lead through the flows, which is a send only if it makes a paid model call. Where a misreading is possible the text says 'send a message' or 'post'. It makes NO model call (the lead's words in Q29 item 1: 'no model call needed'), so it is not a send; it does make two made-up HubSpot contacts (item 5). 1. [OPEN] IN PLAIN WORDS: the lead sends the owner several messages (a message asking the owner to delete one made-up HubSpot contact by hand, called delete message 1 and 2; the lead's statement of the ceiling of sends, that is how many paid model calls the standing yes covers (the dollar figure is a different thing, called the ceiling announcement in item 3; it is not meant here); and others); for each one, what do we do if the owner does not answer? The proposed default for each is written below (for example: no answer to delete message 1 means delete message 2 is not sent). SILENCE RULES (ownersteps7 item 1; ownersteps8 items 3, 7 and 8): what happens when the owner does not answer each of: delete message 1, delete message 2, the statement of the ceiling of sends (no reply is asked, Q25 item 1), the HubSpot token scopes question (owner sheet item 6), the settings-file comparison (owner sheet item 3), the key revocation (owner sheet item 4), the post (owner sheet item 5) and, only if a retake needs a new yes with numbers (that is so if the lead keeps the ceiling at 3 or 4 sends, or for a second retake or a send over the ceiling when it is sized for 5; item 3 point 5), that retake yes (owner sheet item 7)? The audit's proposals: no reply to delete message 1 means delete message 2 is not sent; if a retake does need a new yes with numbers, silence on it means no retake (if the ceiling is sized for 5 sends, one retake needs no new yes, and owner sheet item 7 is reduced to an information message, a follow-up for the owner sheet); silence on the yes for Row 4 (L5, only if it applies) means Row 4 does not run and the take slips by the slip rule of L32 (the audit's proposal); the owner's rule in the owner sheet, "write nothing before 08:00Z", is about the owner reporting a missing lead message; it is not a reply to an ask, so a reply to the Row 4 yes before Wed 06:00Z (11:30 IST) does not conflict with it (owner sheet wording to be checked as a follow-up, not made here); and the post, the clean-up and the key revocation wait; no reply to a GO message by Tue 17 Nov 16:00Z (21:30 IST), the owner's cut-off of item 6 (b) explained in item 83, turns that GO into NO-GO (a NO-GO stands either way). Needed: confirm or correct each, say in which message it is written, and the reply deadline in each delete message. 22. [OPEN] T21 METHOD (the audit, 2026-11-16; FINDINGS.md row F-002): T21 is the test that makes the take database unreachable and checks that the first reply is 503 and that a resend works. The lead's method "REVOKE CONNECT" was tested by the FINDINGS fixer on a throw-away Postgres 16 with made-up data and changed nothing: CONNECT is granted to PUBLIC by default, so the role still opened a new session. The replacement written in FINDINGS.md F-002 (the audit's proposal, not the lead's decision, UNVERIFIED by the lead): ALTER ROLE .. NOLOGIN blocks new sessions; pg_terminate_backend ends the sessions n8n keeps open; ALTER ROLE .. LOGIN restores; T21 is invalid if the first reply is not 503. Questions: does the lead accept this method, and what is the path of the test log (planned by the audit, lead to confirm: the recorder is the lead, at the practice run, Wed 18 Nov from about 06:00Z = 11:30 IST)? Every open item stands in at least one time row below; an item whose parts or files have different times (for example 8, 10, 14, 36, 38, 39, 51, 56, 70, 71, 74, 75 and 76; 76 (e) in group D only) stands in each of its rows, and group D names the governing time of each pair. IST = UTC + 5 h 30 min. Where to write answers and the key to clashing item numbers are in the paragraph before item 1.
  HOW TO READ A ROW: after the time come the item numbers, then what is asked. TAG: GO = the item says that without an answer GO cannot be given or the answer at Tue 14:00Z is NO-GO; STEP n = the item says RUNBOOK step n does not run or runs late; by the one silence rule of the front page (AUDIT PROPOSAL, lead to confirm; fix-q31-13) a TUESDAY step that does not run means the answer at Tue 17 Nov 14:00Z is NO-GO, unless the row says otherwise, and a WEDNESDAY step that does not run means the take does not start that day and the slip rule of L32 applies (item 51 (d)); every Tuesday STEP row below now says its result; NONE = the item says nothing waits on it; UNKNOWN = the item does not say whether the answer is needed for GO or for a step (whether GO depends on it is not stated); it does not mean that no default exists: the IF NOT part of the row still gives the default the item names, and nothing else is invented. IF NOT = what happens when the lead does not answer by that time; 'DEFAULT: none stated; lead decides' (the wording of older versions) means the item names no default; since fix-q31-4 each such item also carries an AUDIT PROPOSAL for what happens when it is unanswered (what the step waits, or that it does not run), marked 'lead to confirm'; nothing in it is a decision of the lead. In every IF NOT of this index, 'no default stated' is followed by the step that does not run, named by its number (fix-q31-11); where a row names no step, the item itself says that nothing waits.

A Wednesday step waits up to 30 minutes or what is left of the Wednesday cap of item 51 (d) (28 minutes), whichever is smaller, after its time; after that it does not run, the take does not start that day, and the slip rule of L32 applies (day by day until Mon 23 Nov 12:00Z). SILENCE LEAVES BOTH TIMING CASES OVER THE LIMIT (fix-q31-15): a re-pin end of Mon 23 Nov 07:00Z gives a take start at 12:07Z in Timing case 1 (7 minutes after the 12:00Z limit) and at 13:02Z in Timing case 2 (07:00Z plus 362 minutes; 62 minutes after the limit).
